Create `packages/cloudrop/src/server/api.test.ts`:

```ts
import { afterEach, beforeEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";
import { CloudropError, fail, ok } from "../errors.ts";
import type { StorageDriver } from "../storage/driver.ts";
import { PUBLIC_URL, serverFixture } from "../testing/server-fixture.ts";
import { allowAnyone, route } from "./route.ts";

const files = route({ accept: "*/*", maxSize: "1MB", authorize: allowAnyone });

beforeEach(() => {
  vi.useFakeTimers({ toFake: ["Date"] });
  vi.setSystemTime(new Date("2026-11-15T12:34:56Z"));
});
afterEach(() => {
  vi.useRealTimers();
});

describe("api.getUrl", () => {
  it("returns the plain public URL, percent-encoded, when the storage has one", async () => {
    const { cloudrop } = serverFixture({ files }, { publicUrl: true });
    expect(await cloudrop.api.getUrl("team files/a+b.png")).toEqual({
      data: { url: `${PUBLIC_URL}/team%20files/a%2Bb.png`, expiresAt: null },
      error: null,
    });
  });

  it("signs a private URL at the start of its window, valid for two windows", async () => {
    const fixture = serverFixture({ files });
    await fixture.storage.put("docs/a.txt", "hello");
    const { data } = await fixture.cloudrop.api.getUrl("docs/a.txt", { expiresIn: 3600 });
    const url = new URL(data?.url ?? "");
    expect({
      date: url.searchParams.get("X-Amz-Date"),
      expires: url.searchParams.get("X-Amz-Expires"),
      expiresAt: data?.expiresAt,
    }).toEqual({ date: "20261115T120000Z", expires: "7200", expiresAt: new Date("2026-11-15T14:00:00Z") });
    expect(await (await fixture.fake.fetch(url)).text()).toBe("hello");
  });

  it("gives everyone in one window the same URL and a new one in the next", async () => {
    const { cloudrop } = serverFixture({ files });
    vi.setSystemTime(new Date("2026-11-15T12:00:00Z"));
    const first = await cloudrop.api.getUrl("a.txt");
    vi.setSystemTime(new Date("2026-11-15T12:59:59Z"));
    const sameWindow = await cloudrop.api.getUrl("a.txt");
    vi.setSystemTime(new Date("2026-11-15T13:00:00Z"));
    const nextWindow = await cloudrop.api.getUrl("a.txt");
    expect(sameWindow.data?.url).toBe(first.data?.url);
    expect(nextWindow.data?.url).not.toBe(first.data?.url);
  });

  it("signs a URL with the download name even on a public bucket, which cannot set it", async () => {
    const fixture = serverFixture({ files }, { publicUrl: true });
    await fixture.storage.put("r/1.pdf", "pdf");
    const { data } = await fixture.cloudrop.api.getUrl("r/1.pdf", { download: "Raport roczny.pdf" });
    const response = await fixture.fake.fetch(data?.url ?? "");
    expect(response.headers.get("content-disposition")).toBe(
      "attachment; filename=\"Raport roczny.pdf\"; filename*=UTF-8''Raport%20roczny.pdf",
    );
  });

  it.for<{ name: string; options: Record<string, unknown>; message: string }>([
    {
      name: "an expiry of 0",
      options: { expiresIn: 0 },
      message: "expiresIn must be a whole number of seconds from 1 to 302400.",
    },
    // Two windows of 302,401 s pass R2's 7-day presign limit.
    {
      name: "an expiry of 302,401 s",
      options: { expiresIn: 302_401 },
      message: "expiresIn must be a whole number of seconds from 1 to 302400.",
    },
    {
      name: "a fractional expiry",
      options: { expiresIn: 1.5 },
      message: "expiresIn must be a whole number of seconds from 1 to 302400.",
    },
    {
      name: "an empty download name",
      options: { download: "" },
      message: 'download must be a file name, such as "report.pdf".',
    },
  ])("refuses $name", async ({ options, message }) => {
    const { cloudrop } = serverFixture({ files });
    expect((await cloudrop.api.getUrl("a.txt", options)).error).toMatchObject({ code: "INVALID_INPUT", message });
  });

  it("accepts the longest window, 302,400 s", async () => {
    const { cloudrop } = serverFixture({ files });
    expect((await cloudrop.api.getUrl("a.txt", { expiresIn: 302_400 })).error).toBeNull();
  });
});

describe("api.deletePrefix", () => {
  it("deletes every page under the prefix, proves it empty, and leaves a neighbouring prefix alone", async () => {
    const fixture = serverFixture({ files });
    // 1,001 keys: one more than a list page, so the second page must be fetched.
    const keys = Array.from({ length: 1001 }, (_, index) => `users/42/${index}.txt`);
    await Promise.all([...keys, "users/420/keep.txt"].map((key) => fixture.storage.put(key, "x")));
    expect(await fixture.cloudrop.api.deletePrefix("users/42/")).toEqual({
      data: { deleted: 1001, verifiedAt: new Date("2026-11-15T12:34:56Z") },
      error: null,
    });
    expect((await fixture.storage.list("users/")).data?.items.map((item) => item.key)).toEqual(["users/420/keep.txt"]);
  });

  it.for<{ name: string; prefix: unknown }>([
    { name: "an empty prefix, which is the whole bucket", prefix: "" },
    // Without the slash it would also match "users/420/".
    { name: "a prefix without a trailing slash", prefix: "users/42" },
    { name: "a lone slash", prefix: "/" },
    { name: "a number", prefix: 42 },
  ])("refuses $name without listing anything", async ({ prefix }) => {
    const fixture = serverFixture({ files });
    expect((await fixture.cloudrop.api.deletePrefix(prefix as string)).error).toMatchObject({
      code: "INVALID_INPUT",
      message: 'deletePrefix takes a folder prefix ending in "/", such as "users/42/".',
    });
    expect(fixture.storageCalls).toEqual([]);
  });

  function withStorage(changes: Partial<StorageDriver>) {
    const base = serverFixture({ files });
    const storage = { ...base.storage, ...changes };
    return { fixture: serverFixture({ files }, { storage }), base };
  }

  it("deletes nothing when the listing returns a key outside the prefix", async () => {
    const deleteMany = vi.fn<StorageDriver["deleteMany"]>();
    const { fixture } = withStorage({
      list: async () =>
        ok({ items: [{ key: "users/7/private.txt", size: 1, etag: "e", uploadedAt: new Date() }], nextCursor: null }),
      deleteMany,
    });
    expect((await fixture.cloudrop.api.deletePrefix("users/42/")).error?.message).toBe(
      "The storage listed keys outside the prefix, so nothing more was deleted.",
    );
    expect(deleteMany).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
  });

  it("fails instead of looping when deleted keys are listed again", async () => {
    const { fixture } = withStorage({
      list: async () =>
        ok({ items: [{ key: "users/42/ghost.txt", size: 1, etag: "e", uploadedAt: new Date() }], nextCursor: null }),
      deleteMany: async (keys) => ok({ deleted: [...keys], failed: [] }),
    });
    expect((await fixture.cloudrop.api.deletePrefix("users/42/")).error).toMatchObject({
      code: "STORAGE_ERROR",
      retryable: true,
      message: "Some keys were still listed after they were deleted. Retry deletePrefix.",
    });
  });

  it("stops after 10,000 pages when keys keep appearing", async () => {
    let page = 0;
    const { fixture } = withStorage({
      list: async () =>
        ok({
          items: [{ key: `users/42/${page++}.txt`, size: 1, etag: "e", uploadedAt: new Date() }],
          nextCursor: null,
        }),
      deleteMany: async (keys) => ok({ deleted: [...keys], failed: [] }),
    });
    expect((await fixture.cloudrop.api.deletePrefix("users/42/")).error?.message).toBe(
      "Objects kept appearing under users/42/ after 10000 pages were deleted. Stop writing there, then retry.",
    );
    expect(page).toBe(10_000);
  });

  it("reports keys it could not delete, with the first one's reason", async () => {
    const denied = new CloudropError("STORAGE_DENIED", "Access denied.");
    const { fixture } = withStorage({
      list: async () =>
        ok({ items: [{ key: "users/42/a.txt", size: 1, etag: "e", uploadedAt: new Date() }], nextCursor: null }),
      deleteMany: async () => ok({ deleted: [], failed: [{ key: "users/42/a.txt", error: denied }] }),
    });
    expect((await fixture.cloudrop.api.deletePrefix("users/42/")).error).toMatchObject({
      code: "STORAGE_ERROR",
      retryable: false,
      message: "Could not delete 1 of 1 keys, such as users/42/a.txt: Access denied.",
    });
  });

  it("passes a failed listing through", async () => {
    const down = new CloudropError("STORAGE_UNREACHABLE", "Could not reach the storage service.", { retryable: true });
    const { fixture } = withStorage({ list: async () => fail(down) });
    expect((await fixture.cloudrop.api.deletePrefix("users/42/")).error).toBe(down);
  });
});

describe("api.checkBucket", () => {
  const uploadRule = (expose: string) =>
    `<CORSRule><AllowedOrigin>https://app.example.com</AllowedOrigin><AllowedMethod>PUT</AllowedMethod><AllowedHeader>content-type</AllowedHeader><AllowedHeader>content-disposition</AllowedHeader><AllowedHeader>if-none-match</AllowedHeader><AllowedHeader>x-amz-meta-*</AllowedHeader>${expose}</CORSRule>`;
  const abortRule =
    "<Rule><ID>abort</ID><Status>Enabled</Status><Filter><Prefix></Prefix></Filter><AbortIncompleteMultipartUpload><DaysAfterInitiation>7</DaysAfterInitiation></AbortIncompleteMultipartUpload></Rule>";

  it("reports a bucket that is ready for browser uploads", async () => {
    const fixture = serverFixture(
      { files },
      {
        trustedOrigins: ["https://app.example.com"],
        bucketConfig: {
          cors: `<CORSConfiguration>${uploadRule("<ExposeHeader>ETag</ExposeHeader>")}</CORSConfiguration>`,
          lifecycle: `<LifecycleConfiguration>${abortRule}</LifecycleConfiguration>`,
        },
      },
    );
    expect((await fixture.cloudrop.api.checkBucket()).data).toEqual({
      cors: { status: "ok", message: "Browsers on https://app.example.com can upload and read ETag." },
      lifecycle: { status: "ok", message: "Incomplete multipart uploads are aborted after 7 days." },
      publicAccess: { status: "ok", message: "No private route stores files on a public bucket." },
    });
  });

  it("finds a rule that does not expose ETag, a missing abort rule and a private route on a public bucket", async () => {
    const privateFiles = route({ accept: "*/*", maxSize: "1MB", authorize: allowAnyone, visibility: "private" });
    const fixture = serverFixture(
      { privateFiles },
      {
        publicUrl: true,
        bucketConfig: {
          cors: `<CORSConfiguration>${uploadRule("")}</CORSConfiguration>`,
          lifecycle: "<LifecycleConfiguration></LifecycleConfiguration>",
        },
      },
    );
    expect((await fixture.cloudrop.api.checkBucket({ origins: ["https://app.example.com"] })).data).toEqual({
      cors: {
        status: "problem",
        message:
          "The bucket's CORS rules do not let https://app.example.com upload. Add a rule that allows PUT from there with the headers content-type, content-disposition, if-none-match, x-amz-meta-cloudrop-id and exposes ETag.",
      },
      lifecycle: {
        status: "problem",
        message:
          "No enabled lifecycle rule aborts incomplete multipart uploads across the bucket, so abandoned uploads keep their parts stored. Add one (R2 creates a 7-day rule by default).",
      },
      publicAccess: {
        status: "problem",
        message:
          "Private routes (privateFiles) store files on a bucket with a public URL: anyone who learns a key can read the file.",
      },
    });
  });

  it("reports unknown, never missing, when the credentials may not read the settings", async () => {
    const { cloudrop } = serverFixture({ files });
    const report = await cloudrop.api.checkBucket({ origins: ["https://app.example.com"] });
    expect([report.data?.cors.status, report.data?.lifecycle.status]).toEqual(["unknown", "unknown"]);
  });

  it("asks for origins when it has none to check", async () => {
    const { cloudrop } = serverFixture({ files }, { bucketConfig: {} });
    expect((await cloudrop.api.checkBucket()).data?.cors).toEqual({
      status: "unknown",
      message: 'Name the origins your pages upload from: api.checkBucket({ origins: ["https://app.example.com"] }).',
    });
  });
});

describe("development start-up warnings", () => {
  afterEach(() => {
    vi.unstubAllEnvs();
  });

  it("checks the bucket once, on the first request in development, and logs each problem", async () => {
    vi.stubEnv("NODE_ENV", "development");
    const fixture = serverFixture(
      { files },
      {
        trustedOrigins: ["https://app.example.com"],
        bucketConfig: { cors: "<CORSConfiguration></CORSConfiguration>" },
      },
    );
    await fixture.post("upload", { route: "files", files: [{ name: "a.txt", size: 1, type: "text/plain" }] });
    await fixture.post("upload", { route: "files", files: [{ name: "b.txt", size: 1, type: "text/plain" }] });
    await vi.waitFor(() => expect(fixture.logger.warnings).toHaveLength(2));
    expect(fixture.logger.warnings).toEqual([
      "cloudrop: The bucket's CORS rules do not let https://app.example.com upload. Add a rule that allows PUT from there with the headers content-type, content-disposition, if-none-match, x-amz-meta-cloudrop-id and exposes ETag.",
      "cloudrop: No enabled lifecycle rule aborts incomplete multipart uploads across the bucket, so abandoned uploads keep their parts stored. Add one (R2 creates a 7-day rule by default).",
    ]);
    // The two reads run in parallel, so their order is not part of the contract.
    expect(
      fixture.storageCalls.filter((call) => call.endsWith("?cors") || call.endsWith("?lifecycle")).toSorted(),
    ).toEqual(["GET /uploads?cors", "GET /uploads?lifecycle"]);
  });

  it("does not check the bucket outside development", async () => {
    vi.stubEnv("NODE_ENV", "production");
    const fixture = serverFixture({ files });
    await fixture.post("upload", { route: "files", files: [{ name: "a.txt", size: 1, type: "text/plain" }] });
    expect(fixture.storageCalls).toEqual([]);
  });
});
```

Bugs these tests catch, beyond the obvious ones:

- A URL signed with the current second instead of the window start: no two viewers share a URL (no cache hits).
- A window over 302,400 s: two windows would pass R2's 7-day limit.
- `deletePrefix("users/42")` also erasing `users/420/`; `deletePrefix("")` erasing the bucket.
- Deleting listed keys outside the prefix; looping forever when deletes do not stick or writers keep adding keys.
- A failed listing reported as an empty prefix.
- A rule without `ExposeHeaders: ETag` reported as fine: browsers could not read part ETags.
- "unknown" reported as "problem" (or "ok") when the token may not read the setting.
- Checking the bucket on every request, or outside development.

`deletePrefix`, `checkBucket`) because `cloudrop.api` does not exist yet, and the development-warning case because no
bucket read is made. "does not check the bucket outside development" already passes: nothing checks the bucket yet.

Create `packages/cloudrop/src/server/bucket-check.ts`:

```ts
// api.checkBucket and the development start-up warnings (spec §4, §9, §12): bucket settings are verified by code,
// never assumed from the docs.

import { fail, ok, type Result } from "../errors.ts";
import type { BucketCorsRule, BucketLifecycleRule } from "../storage/driver.ts";
import type { ServerConfig } from "./config.ts";

export interface BucketCheck {
  /** "unknown": the credentials may not read this setting (R2 object tokens cannot), or nothing was given to check. */
  status: "ok" | "problem" | "unknown";
  message: string;
}

export interface BucketReport {
  cors: BucketCheck;
  lifecycle: BucketCheck;
  publicAccess: BucketCheck;
}

/** The headers cloudrop's signed PUTs carry (spec §7.5); a preflight fails if the bucket's CORS rule omits one. */
const UPLOAD_HEADERS = ["content-type", "content-disposition", "if-none-match", "x-amz-meta-cloudrop-id"];

/** S3 CORS patterns may hold one "*" wildcard, such as "https://*.example.com" or "x-amz-meta-*". */
function matchesAny(patterns: readonly string[], value: string): boolean {
  return patterns.some((pattern) => {
    const [head = "", tail] = pattern.toLowerCase().split("*");
    if (tail === undefined) return head === value;
    return value.length >= head.length + tail.length && value.startsWith(head) && value.endsWith(tail);
  });
}

const allowsUploads = (rule: BucketCorsRule, origin: string): boolean =>
  matchesAny(rule.allowedOrigins, origin) &&
  rule.allowedMethods.includes("PUT") &&
  UPLOAD_HEADERS.every((header) => matchesAny(rule.allowedHeaders, header)) &&
  rule.exposeHeaders.some((header) => header.toLowerCase() === "etag");

function corsCheck(rules: readonly BucketCorsRule[], origins: readonly string[]): BucketCheck {
  if (origins.length === 0) {
    return {
      status: "unknown",
      message: 'Name the origins your pages upload from: api.checkBucket({ origins: ["https://app.example.com"] }).',
    };
  }
  const blocked = origins.filter((origin) => !rules.some((rule) => allowsUploads(rule, origin.toLowerCase())));
  if (blocked.length === 0)
    return { status: "ok", message: `Browsers on ${origins.join(", ")} can upload and read ETag.` };
  return {
    status: "problem",
    message: `The bucket's CORS rules do not let ${blocked.join(", ")} upload. Add a rule that allows PUT from there with the headers ${UPLOAD_HEADERS.join(", ")} and exposes ETag.`,
  };
}

function lifecycleCheck(rules: readonly BucketLifecycleRule[]): BucketCheck {
  const rule = rules.find(
    (candidate) =>
      candidate.enabled && candidate.prefix === "" && candidate.abortIncompleteMultipartUploadDays !== undefined,
  );
  if (rule) {
    return {
      status: "ok",
      message: `Incomplete multipart uploads are aborted after ${rule.abortIncompleteMultipartUploadDays} days.`,
    };
  }
  return {
    status: "problem",
    message:
      "No enabled lifecycle rule aborts incomplete multipart uploads across the bucket, so abandoned uploads keep their parts stored. Add one (R2 creates a 7-day rule by default).",
  };
}

const unreadable = (setting: string): BucketCheck => ({
  status: "unknown",
  message: `These storage credentials may not read the bucket's ${setting} (R2 object tokens cannot). Check it with an admin read token or in the dashboard.`,
});

export async function checkBucket(config: ServerConfig, origins: readonly string[]): Promise<Result<BucketReport>> {
  const { storage } = config;
  const [cors, lifecycle] = await Promise.all([storage.getBucketCors(), storage.getBucketLifecycle()]);
  for (const result of [cors, lifecycle]) {
    if (result.error && result.error.code !== "STORAGE_DENIED") return fail(result.error);
  }
  const privateRoutes = [...config.routes.values()].filter((route) => route.visibility === "private");
  const publicAccess: BucketCheck =
    privateRoutes.length > 0 && storage.publicUrl
      ? {
          status: "problem",
          message: `Private routes (${privateRoutes.map((route) => route.name).join(", ")}) store files on a bucket with a public URL: anyone who learns a key can read the file.`,
        }
      : { status: "ok", message: "No private route stores files on a public bucket." };
  return ok({
    cors: cors.data ? corsCheck(cors.data, origins) : unreadable("CORS rules"),
    lifecycle: lifecycle.data ? lifecycleCheck(lifecycle.data) : unreadable("lifecycle rules"),
    publicAccess,
  });
}
```

Create `packages/cloudrop/src/server/api.ts`:

```ts
// cloudrop.api (spec §9): server-side file operations on the configured storage, all returning { data, error }.

import { CloudropError, fail, ok, type Result } from "../errors.ts";
import type { ObjectInfo, ObjectOptions, StorageDriver } from "../storage/driver.ts";
import { encodeKeyPath } from "../storage/sigv4.ts";
import { keyProblem, prefixProblem } from "../storage/validate.ts";
import { type BucketReport, checkBucket } from "./bucket-check.ts";
import type { ServerConfig } from "./config.ts";
import { invalidInput } from "./http.ts";
import { contentDisposition } from "./key.ts";
import { nowSeconds } from "./presign.ts";

export interface FileApi {
  /**
   * A URL to read the object. With the storage's publicUrl and no options: the plain public URL. Otherwise a presigned
   * GET signed at the start of an `expiresIn` window (default 3600 s) and valid for two windows, so every viewer in a
   * window gets the same cacheable URL, valid for at least `expiresIn` more seconds. `download` names the saved file.
   */
  getUrl(
    key: string,
    options?: { expiresIn?: number; download?: string },
  ): Promise<Result<{ url: string; expiresAt: Date | null }>>;
  /** `null` only when the object does not exist; a network failure is an error, never "not found". */
  head(key: string): Promise<Result<ObjectInfo | null>>;
  /** A small server-side upload, up to the storage's single-PUT limit. */
  put(
    key: string,
    body: Uint8Array<ArrayBuffer> | ArrayBuffer | string | Blob,
    options?: ObjectOptions,
  ): Promise<Result<{ etag: string }>>;
  delete(key: string): Promise<Result<void>>;
  /** Any number of keys, one request per key, 16 at a time; each key is reported as deleted or failed. */
  deleteMany(
    keys: readonly string[],
  ): Promise<Result<{ deleted: string[]; failed: { key: string; error: CloudropError }[] }>>;
  copy(from: string, to: string): Promise<Result<{ etag: string }>>;
  /**
   * Deletes everything under a folder prefix ending in "/", page by page, then lists it again: `verifiedAt` is when the
   * listing came back empty. For erasure requests.
   */
  deletePrefix(prefix: string): Promise<Result<{ deleted: number; verifiedAt: Date }>>;
  /** CORS for `origins` (default: trustedOrigins), the abort-incomplete lifecycle rule, and public-domain conflicts. */
  checkBucket(options?: { origins?: readonly string[] }): Promise<Result<BucketReport>>;
}

const DEFAULT_URL_WINDOW_SECONDS = 3600;
/** 10,000 pages of 1,000 keys: far beyond any one prefix, and a bound if writers keep adding keys while it deletes. */
const MAX_DELETE_PAGES = 10_000;

async function deletePrefix(
  storage: StorageDriver,
  prefix: string,
): Promise<Result<{ deleted: number; verifiedAt: Date }>> {
  // Without the trailing slash, "users/4" would also delete "users/42/".
  if (typeof prefix !== "string" || !prefix.endsWith("/") || prefix === "/") {
    return fail(invalidInput('deletePrefix takes a folder prefix ending in "/", such as "users/42/".'));
  }
  const problem = prefixProblem(prefix);
  if (problem) return fail(invalidInput(problem));
  let deleted = 0;
  let previous = new Set<string>();
  for (let page = 0; page < MAX_DELETE_PAGES; page++) {
    const listed = await storage.list(prefix);
    if (listed.error) return listed;
    const keys = listed.data.items.map((item) => item.key);
    if (keys.length === 0) return ok({ deleted, verifiedAt: new Date() });
    // A listing that strays outside the prefix is a storage or encoding fault: deleting it could erase other data.
    if (keys.some((key) => !key.startsWith(prefix))) {
      return fail(
        new CloudropError("STORAGE_ERROR", "The storage listed keys outside the prefix, so nothing more was deleted."),
      );
    }
    if (keys.some((key) => previous.has(key))) {
      return fail(
        new CloudropError("STORAGE_ERROR", "Some keys were still listed after they were deleted. Retry deletePrefix.", {
          retryable: true,
        }),
      );
    }
    const removed = await storage.deleteMany(keys);
    if (removed.error) return removed;
    const [first] = removed.data.failed;
    if (first) {
      return fail(
        new CloudropError(
          "STORAGE_ERROR",
          `Could not delete ${removed.data.failed.length} of ${keys.length} keys, such as ${first.key}: ${first.error.message}`,
          { retryable: first.error.retryable, cause: first.error },
        ),
      );
    }
    deleted += keys.length;
    previous = new Set(keys);
  }
  return fail(
    new CloudropError(
      "STORAGE_ERROR",
      `Objects kept appearing under ${prefix} after ${MAX_DELETE_PAGES} pages were deleted. Stop writing there, then retry.`,
    ),
  );
}

export function createFileApi(config: ServerConfig): FileApi {
  const { storage } = config;
  return {
    async getUrl(key, options) {
      const { expiresIn, download } = options ?? {};
      if (storage.publicUrl && expiresIn === undefined && download === undefined) {
        const problem = keyProblem(key);
        return problem
          ? fail(invalidInput(problem))
          : ok({ url: `${storage.publicUrl}/${encodeKeyPath(key)}`, expiresAt: null });
      }
      const window = expiresIn ?? DEFAULT_URL_WINDOW_SECONDS;
      const maxWindow = Math.floor(storage.rules.maxPresignSeconds / 2);
      if (!Number.isInteger(window) || window < 1 || window > maxWindow) {
        return fail(invalidInput(`expiresIn must be a whole number of seconds from 1 to ${maxWindow}.`));
      }
      if (download !== undefined && (typeof download !== "string" || download === "" || !download.isWellFormed())) {
        return fail(invalidInput('download must be a file name, such as "report.pdf".'));
      }
      const windowStart = Math.floor(nowSeconds() / window) * window;
      return storage.presignGet(key, {
        expiresIn: 2 * window,
        signedAt: new Date(windowStart * 1000),
        disposition: download === undefined ? undefined : contentDisposition("application/octet-stream", download),
      });
    },
    head: (key) => storage.head(key),
    put: (key, body, options) => storage.put(key, body, options),
    delete: (key) => storage.delete(key),
    deleteMany: (keys) => storage.deleteMany(keys),
    copy: (from, to) => storage.copy(from, to),
    deletePrefix: (prefix) => deletePrefix(storage, prefix),
    checkBucket: (options) => checkBucket(config, options?.origins ?? [...config.trustedOrigins]),
  };
}
```

```ts
}

/** In development only, once per handler: report bucket settings that would break browser uploads (spec §4). */
async function warnAboutBucket(config: ServerConfig): Promise<void> {
  const report = await checkBucket(config, [...config.trustedOrigins]);
  if (report.error) {
    config.logger.warn(`cloudrop: could not check the bucket settings: ${report.error.message}`);
    return;
  }
  for (const check of Object.values(report.data)) {
    if (check.status === "problem") config.logger.warn(`cloudrop: ${check.message}`);
  }
}
```

```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { toNextHandler } from "./next.ts";
import { allowAnyone, route } from "./server/route.ts";
import { APP_ORIGIN, serverFixture } from "./testing/server-fixture.ts";

describe("toNextHandler", () => {
  it("answers uploads, preflights and wrong methods through the same handler", async () => {
    const { cloudrop } = serverFixture(
      { files: route({ accept: "*/*", maxSize: "1MB", authorize: allowAnyone }) },
      { trustedOrigins: ["https://admin.example.com"] },
    );
    const { GET, POST, OPTIONS } = toNextHandler(cloudrop);
    const url = `${APP_ORIGIN}/api/cloudrop/upload`;
    const posted = await POST(
      new Request(url, {
        method: "POST",
        headers: { "content-type": "application/json" },
        body: JSON.stringify({ route: "files", files: [{ name: "a.txt", size: 1, type: "text/plain" }] }),
      }),
    );
    const preflight = await OPTIONS(
      new Request(url, {
        method: "OPTIONS",
        headers: { origin: "https://admin.example.com", "access-control-request-method": "POST" },
      }),
    );
    expect([posted.status, preflight.status, (await GET(new Request(url))).status]).toEqual([200, 204, 405]);
  });
});
```

Create `packages/cloudrop/src/node.test.ts`:

```ts
import { once } from "node:events";
import { createServer, type Server } from "node:http";
import type { AddressInfo } from "node:net";
import { afterEach, describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { toNodeHandler } from "./node.ts";
import { allowAnyone, route } from "./server/route.ts";
import { serverFixture } from "./testing/server-fixture.ts";

let server: Server | undefined;
afterEach(async () => {
  server?.close();
  if (server) await once(server, "close");
  server = undefined;
});

/** A real node:http server on a free loopback port. Assigning the adapter here is the type test: real
 * IncomingMessage and ServerResponse values must fit its hand-written interfaces. */
async function listen(trustedOrigins: string[] = []): Promise<string> {
  const { cloudrop } = serverFixture(
    { files: route({ accept: "*/*", maxSize: "1MB", authorize: allowAnyone }) },
    { trustedOrigins },
  );
  server = createServer(toNodeHandler(cloudrop));
  server.listen(0, "127.0.0.1");
  await once(server, "listening");
  return `http://127.0.0.1:${(server.address() as AddressInfo).port}`;
}

const body = JSON.stringify({ route: "files", files: [{ name: "a.txt", size: 1, type: "text/plain" }] });

describe("toNodeHandler", () => {
  it("answers an upload with the handler's status, headers and JSON", async () => {
    const base = await listen();
    const response = await fetch(`${base}/api/cloudrop/upload`, {
      method: "POST",
      headers: { "content-type": "application/json", origin: base },
      body,
    });
    const json = (await response.json()) as { files: { kind: string }[] };
    expect([response.status, response.headers.get("content-type"), json.files[0]?.kind]).toEqual([
      200,
      "application/json",
      "single",
    ]);
  });

  it("refuses another origin: the request URL comes from the Host header, not from the caller", async () => {
    const base = await listen();
    const response = await fetch(`${base}/api/cloudrop/upload`, {
      method: "POST",
      headers: { "content-type": "application/json", origin: "http://evil.example" },
      body,
    });
    expect(response.status).toBe(403);
  });

  it("answers 413 to a 100 KiB body and still delivers the answer", async () => {
    const base = await listen();
    // Valid JSON, so a body read in full would get 200: only the cap can answer 413.
    const large = JSON.stringify({
      route: "files",
      files: [{ name: "a.txt", size: 1, type: "text/plain" }],
      pad: "x".repeat(100 * 1024),
    });
    const response = await fetch(`${base}/api/cloudrop/upload`, {
      method: "POST",
      headers: { "content-type": "application/json" },
      body: large,
    });
    expect([response.status, ((await response.json()) as { error: { code: string } }).error.code]).toEqual([
      413,
      "BODY_TOO_LARGE",
    ]);
  });

  it("reads at most one chunk past 64 KiB of a 4 MiB body, so a large request cannot fill memory", async () => {
    let received = 0;
    server = createServer(
      toNodeHandler({
        handler: async (request) => {
          received = (await request.arrayBuffer()).byteLength;
          return new Response(null, { status: 413 });
        },
      }),
    );
    server.listen(0, "127.0.0.1");
    await once(server, "listening");
    const response = await fetch(`http://127.0.0.1:${(server.address() as AddressInfo).port}/api/cloudrop/upload`, {
      method: "POST",
      body: new Uint8Array(4 * 1024 * 1024),
    });
    expect(response.status).toBe(413);
    // Node delivers a request body in chunks of at most 64 KiB: past the cap, one more chunk at most.
    expect(received).toBeGreaterThan(65_536);
    expect(received).toBeLessThanOrEqual(2 * 65_536);
  });

  it("passes CORS preflights through to the handler", async () => {
    const base = await listen(["https://admin.example.com"]);
    const response = await fetch(`${base}/api/cloudrop/upload`, {
      method: "OPTIONS",
      headers: { origin: "https://admin.example.com", "access-control-request-method": "POST" },
    });
    expect([response.status, response.headers.get("access-control-allow-origin")]).toEqual([
      204,
      "https://admin.example.com",
    ]);
  });
});
```

Bugs these tests catch, beyond the obvious ones:

- An adapter that reads the whole request before the cap: a 4 MiB body would be buffered ("reads at most one chunk
  past 64 KiB…"; reading everything still answers 413, so only this test sees it).
- Breaking out of the body iterator: Node destroys the socket and the 413 never arrives.
- Taking the scheme or host from `X-Forwarded-*`, or treating any origin as same-origin.
- Dropping the handler's headers (CORS, `content-type`) on the way to `ServerResponse`.

- [ ] **Step 2: Run them and watch them fail**

Expected: FAIL. Vitest reports `Error: Cannot find module './next.ts' imported from …` for `next.test.ts`, and `Tests  no tests`. The same for `./node.ts` in `node.test.ts`.

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

Create `packages/cloudrop/src/next.ts`:

```ts
// cloudrop/next: `export const { GET, POST, OPTIONS } = toNextHandler(cloudrop)` in app/api/cloudrop/[...all]/route.ts.

/** The one method of a Cloudrop the adapters call, so this entry imports no server code. */
export interface RequestHandler {
  handler(request: Request): Promise<Response>;
}

export interface NextRouteHandlers {
  GET(request: Request): Promise<Response>;
  POST(request: Request): Promise<Response>;
  /** CORS preflights from trustedOrigins. */
  OPTIONS(request: Request): Promise<Response>;
}

export function toNextHandler(cloudrop: RequestHandler): NextRouteHandlers {
  const handle = (request: Request) => cloudrop.handler(request);
  return { GET: handle, POST: handle, OPTIONS: handle };
}
```

Create `packages/cloudrop/src/node.ts`:

```ts
// cloudrop/node: `http.createServer(toNodeHandler(cloudrop))`, or mount it in Express or Fastify's raw handler.
// The library compiles without Node's types (tsconfig `types: []`), so the two Node objects are described by the
// members used here; node.test.ts proves real IncomingMessage and ServerResponse values fit them.

import { MAX_BODY_BYTES } from "./protocol/types.ts";

/** The parts of Node's IncomingMessage this adapter reads. */
export interface NodeRequest extends AsyncIterable<Uint8Array> {
  method?: string;
  url?: string;
  headers: Record<string, string | string[] | undefined>;
  socket: object;
}

/** The parts of Node's ServerResponse this adapter writes. */
export interface NodeResponse {
  statusCode: number;
  setHeader(name: string, value: string): unknown;
  end(body: Uint8Array): unknown;
}

/** The one method of a Cloudrop the adapters call, so this entry imports no server code. */
export interface RequestHandler {
  handler(request: Request): Promise<Response>;
}

/**
 * Reads at most one chunk past the cap, so the handler sees an oversized body and answers 413. The iterator is not
 * closed: closing it would destroy the socket before the answer is sent, and Node discards the unread rest itself.
 */
async function readBody(request: NodeRequest): Promise<Uint8Array<ArrayBuffer>> {
  const iterator = request[Symbol.asyncIterator]();
  const chunks: Uint8Array[] = [];
  let length = 0;
  while (length <= MAX_BODY_BYTES) {
    const chunk = await iterator.next();
    if (chunk.done) break;
    chunks.push(chunk.value);
    length += chunk.value.length;
  }
  const body = new Uint8Array(length);
  let offset = 0;
  for (const chunk of chunks) {
    body.set(chunk, offset);
    offset += chunk.length;
  }
  return body;
}

function toUrl(request: NodeRequest): URL {
  // No X-Forwarded-* header is trusted: behind a proxy, list the public origin in trustedOrigins instead.
  const scheme = "encrypted" in request.socket && request.socket.encrypted === true ? "https" : "http";
  const host = [request.headers.host].flat()[0] ?? "localhost";
  const base = URL.canParse(`${scheme}://${host}`) ? `${scheme}://${host}` : `${scheme}://localhost`;
  return new URL(request.url ?? "/", base);
}

export function toNodeHandler(
  cloudrop: RequestHandler,
): (request: NodeRequest, response: NodeResponse) => Promise<void> {
  return async (request, response) => {
    const headers = new Headers();
    for (const [name, value] of Object.entries(request.headers)) {
      // HTTP/2 pseudo-headers (":authority") are not header names a Request accepts.
      if (name.startsWith(":") || value === undefined) continue;
      for (const item of [value].flat()) headers.append(name, item);
    }
    const method = request.method ?? "GET";
    const body = method === "GET" || method === "HEAD" ? undefined : await readBody(request);
    const answer = await cloudrop.handler(new Request(toUrl(request), { method, headers, body }));
    response.statusCode = answer.status;
    answer.headers.forEach((value, name) => {
      response.setHeader(name, value);
    });
    response.end(new Uint8Array(await answer.arrayBuffer()));
  };
}
```

### Task 15: The upload protocol end to end on the fake R2

The whole protocol, driven the way M3's client will drive it: JSON POSTs to the four endpoints and PUTs that send
exactly the URL and headers the server returned. Single, multipart with resume, URL renewal, abort and several files per
call.

**Files:**
- Test: `packages/cloudrop/src/server/protocol.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `serverFixture`, `offsetBytes`, `route`, the protocol types.
- Produces: no code.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the end-to-end tests**

Create `packages/cloudrop/src/server/protocol.test.ts`:

```ts
// The whole protocol on the in-process fake R2, driven the way M3's client will drive it: JSON POSTs to the four
// endpoints, and PUTs that send exactly the URL and headers the server returned.

import { afterEach, beforeEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";
import type {
  CompleteResponse,
  ErrorResponse,
  MultipartTicket,
  SignedPart,
  SignResponse,
  SingleTicket,
  UploadResponse,
} from "../protocol/types.ts";
import { MiB } from "../rules.ts";
import { serverFixture } from "../testing/server-fixture.ts";
import { offsetBytes } from "../testing/storage-contract.ts";
import { route } from "./route.ts";

beforeEach(() => {
  vi.useFakeTimers({ toFake: ["Date"] });
  vi.setSystemTime(new Date("2026-11-15T12:00:00Z"));
});
afterEach(() => {
  vi.useRealTimers();
});

const albums = route({
  accept: ["image/png", "application/octet-stream"],
  maxSize: "100MB",
  authorize: ({ request }) => ({ userId: request.headers.get("x-user") ?? "anonymous" }),
  prefix: ({ userId }) => `users/${userId}`,
  onComplete: ({ userId, file }) => ({ owner: userId, bytes: file.size }),
});

function client() {
  const fixture = serverFixture({ albums }, { multipartThreshold: "8MB" });
  let requests = 0;
  async function call<T>(
    endpoint: string,
    body: unknown,
  ): Promise<{ status: number; body: T & Partial<ErrorResponse> }> {
    requests += 1;
    const response = await fixture.post(endpoint, body, { "x-user": "42" });
    return { status: response.status, body: (await response.json()) as T & Partial<ErrorResponse> };
  }
  async function put(signed: { url: string; headers: Record<string, string> }, bytes: Uint8Array<ArrayBuffer>) {
    requests += 1;
    return (await fixture.send(signed, bytes)).status;
  }
  return { fixture, call, put, requests: () => requests };
}

describe("upload protocol, end to end", () => {
  it("uploads a small file in three requests: upload, PUT, complete", async () => {
    const { fixture, call, put, requests } = client();
    const bytes = offsetBytes(1000);
    const upload = await call<UploadResponse>("upload", {
      route: "albums",
      files: [{ name: "pic.png", size: 1000, type: "image/png" }],
    });
    const ticket = upload.body.files[0] as SingleTicket;
    expect(await put(ticket.put, bytes)).toBe(200);
    const done = await call<CompleteResponse>("complete", { token: ticket.token });
    expect(done.body).toEqual({
      file: {
        id: ticket.fileId,
        key: `users/42/${ticket.fileId}.png`,
        name: "pic.png",
        size: 1000,
        type: "image/png",
        url: null,
      },
      data: { owner: "42", bytes: 1000 },
    });
    expect(requests()).toBe(3);
    expect((await fixture.storage.getRange(ticket.key, 0, 999)).data).toEqual(bytes);
  });

  it("resumes a multipart upload: complete names the missing part, sign renews it, complete succeeds", async () => {
    const { fixture, call, put } = client();
    const size = 16 * MiB + 5;
    const bytes = offsetBytes(size);
    const upload = await call<UploadResponse>("upload", {
      route: "albums",
      files: [{ name: "video.bin", size, type: "application/octet-stream" }],
    });
    const ticket = upload.body.files[0] as MultipartTicket;
    const [first, , third] = ticket.parts as [SignedPart, SignedPart, SignedPart];
    expect(await put(first, bytes.slice(0, 8 * MiB))).toBe(200);
    expect(await put(third, bytes.slice(16 * MiB))).toBe(200);
    // The page reloads here; the client kept the token and asks the server what is missing.
    const incomplete = await call<CompleteResponse>("complete", { token: ticket.token });
    expect({ status: incomplete.status, parts: incomplete.body.error?.parts }).toEqual({ status: 409, parts: [2] });
    const renewed = await call<SignResponse>("sign", { token: ticket.token, parts: incomplete.body.error?.parts });
    const [second] = (renewed.body as { parts: SignedPart[] }).parts;
    expect(await put(second as SignedPart, bytes.slice(8 * MiB, 16 * MiB))).toBe(200);
    const done = await call<CompleteResponse>("complete", { token: ticket.token });
    expect(done.body.data).toEqual({ owner: "42", bytes: size });
    // Bytes on both sides of each part boundary, in order.
    expect((await fixture.storage.getRange(ticket.key, 8 * MiB - 2, 8 * MiB + 1)).data).toEqual(
      bytes.slice(8 * MiB - 2, 8 * MiB + 2),
    );
    expect((await fixture.storage.getRange(ticket.key, 16 * MiB - 2, 16 * MiB + 1)).data).toEqual(
      bytes.slice(16 * MiB - 2, 16 * MiB + 2),
    );
  });

  it("renews an expired URL through /sign", async () => {
    const { call, put } = client();
    const upload = await call<UploadResponse>("upload", {
      route: "albums",
      files: [{ name: "pic.png", size: 10, type: "image/png" }],
    });
    const ticket = upload.body.files[0] as SingleTicket;
    vi.setSystemTime(new Date("2026-11-15T12:15:01Z"));
    // R2 answers an expired URL with 403 ExpiredRequest (spike row 7); the client renews once and retries.
    expect(await put(ticket.put, offsetBytes(10))).toBe(403);
    const renewed = await call<{ put: SingleTicket["put"] }>("sign", { token: ticket.token });
    expect(await put(renewed.body.put, offsetBytes(10))).toBe(200);
    expect((await call<CompleteResponse>("complete", { token: ticket.token })).status).toBe(200);
  });

  it("aborts: the upload is gone and can no longer complete", async () => {
    const { fixture, call } = client();
    const upload = await call<UploadResponse>("upload", {
      route: "albums",
      files: [{ name: "video.bin", size: 9 * MiB, type: "application/octet-stream" }],
    });
    const ticket = upload.body.files[0] as MultipartTicket;
    expect((await call("abort", { token: ticket.token })).status).toBe(200);
    expect(fixture.fake.pendingUploads()).toBe(0);
    expect((await call<CompleteResponse>("complete", { token: ticket.token })).body.error?.code).toBe("NO_SUCH_UPLOAD");
  });

  it("starts several files in one /upload call, each with its own token", async () => {
    const { call } = client();
    const upload = await call<UploadResponse>("upload", {
      route: "albums",
      files: [
        { name: "a.png", size: 1, type: "image/png" },
        { name: "b.png", size: 2, type: "image/png" },
      ],
    });
    const [a, b] = upload.body.files as [SingleTicket, SingleTicket];
    expect([a.kind, b.kind, a.token === b.token, a.key === b.key]).toEqual(["single", "single", false, false]);
  });
});
```

Bugs these tests catch, beyond the obvious ones:

- Part boundaries that drift between `/upload`, `/sign` and `/complete` (the bytes around each boundary are compared).
- A small file needing more than three requests.
- An expired URL that `/sign` cannot renew.
- An aborted upload that still completes.

Every endpoint exists, so these pass at once. They earn their place by crossing endpoints; to see one go red, run the

`partNumber <= count ? token.partSize : …`, run the command below and watch the resume case fail with 403, then
restore the line (`git diff` empty).

Run: `cd packages/cloudrop && bunx vitest run --project unit src/server/protocol.test.ts`

Expected: PASS, 5 tests.

Run from the repository root:

```bash

```

### Task 16: The live R2 server lane

The protocol through the real handler, with presigned uploads to the `cloudrop-test` bucket over real HTTP, under the
existing safety harness.

**Files:**
- Modify: `scripts/test-live-r2.ts`
- Test: `packages/cloudrop/src/server/handler.r2.integration.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `createCloudrop`, `route`, `allowAnyone`, `r2`, `randomPrefix`, `tearDownRun`, `offsetBytes`,
  `tokenPayload`, `toHex`.
- Produces: the lane file and its place in `bun run test:live`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the lane**

The lane fails in `beforeAll` when a credential is missing. Every key is under the run prefix (the route's `prefix`
returns it), teardown aborts the multipart uploads it opened and deletes the prefix through `tearDownRun`, which refuses
any prefix `randomPrefix` did not make. The secret is random per run.

Create `packages/cloudrop/src/server/handler.r2.integration.test.ts`:

```ts
// The upload protocol against a real R2 bucket: cloudrop.handler signs, the test sends the bytes to R2 over real HTTP,
// as a browser would. Run it with `bun run test:live`, which loads the credentials and redacts them from the output.
// Everything is written under a fresh live-test/cloudrop-<32 hex>/ prefix and deleted by the contract teardown.

import { afterAll, beforeAll, describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import type {
  CompleteResponse,
  ErrorResponse,
  MultipartTicket,
  SignedPart,
  SignResponse,
  SingleTicket,
  UploadResponse,
} from "../protocol/types.ts";
import { MiB } from "../rules.ts";
import type { StorageDriver } from "../storage/driver.ts";
import { r2 } from "../storage/presets.ts";
import { toHex } from "../storage/sigv4.ts";
import { tokenPayload } from "../testing/server-fixture.ts";
import { offsetBytes, randomPrefix, tearDownRun } from "../testing/storage-contract.ts";
import { type Cloudrop, createCloudrop } from "./cloudrop.ts";
import { allowAnyone, route } from "./route.ts";

const SETTINGS = [
  "CLOUDROP_R2_ACCOUNT_ID",
  "CLOUDROP_R2_BUCKET",
  "CLOUDROP_R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID",
  "CLOUDROP_R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY",
] as const;
const ORIGIN = "https://app.example.com";

describe("upload protocol on live R2", () => {
  const prefix = randomPrefix("live-test/");
  const openUploads: { key: string; uploadId: string }[] = [];
  let storage: StorageDriver | undefined;
  let cloudrop: Cloudrop;

  beforeAll(() => {
    const missing = SETTINGS.filter((name) => !process.env[name]);
    if (missing.length > 0) {
      throw new Error(
        `Live R2 settings are missing: ${missing.join(", ")}. Run \`bun run test:live\` with CLOUDROP_R2_ENV_FILE naming a file that sets them, or export them.`,
      );
    }
    storage = r2({ bucket: process.env.CLOUDROP_R2_BUCKET as string });
    cloudrop = createCloudrop({
      storage,
      // A fresh secret per run: no token from this run is valid anywhere else.
      secret: toHex(crypto.getRandomValues(new Uint8Array(32))),
      // Every key lands under the run prefix, which teardown deletes.
      routes: { live: route({ accept: "*/*", maxSize: "100MB", authorize: allowAnyone, prefix: () => prefix }) },
      multipartThreshold: "8MB",
    });
  });

  afterAll(() => tearDownRun(storage && { driver: storage, prefix, openUploads }, []));

  async function call<T>(
    endpoint: string,
    body: unknown,
  ): Promise<{ status: number; body: T & Partial<ErrorResponse> }> {
    const response = await cloudrop.handler(
      new Request(`${ORIGIN}/api/cloudrop/${endpoint}`, {
        method: "POST",
        headers: { "content-type": "application/json", origin: ORIGIN },
        body: JSON.stringify(body),
      }),
    );
    return { status: response.status, body: (await response.json()) as T & Partial<ErrorResponse> };
  }

  async function put(signed: { url: string; headers: Record<string, string> }, bytes: Uint8Array<ArrayBuffer>) {
    const response = await fetch(signed.url, { method: "PUT", headers: signed.headers, body: bytes });
    await response.arrayBuffer();
    return response.status;
  }

  it("uploads a single file with the signed headers R2 stores, and serves it through a signed download URL", async () => {
    const upload = await call<UploadResponse>("upload", {
      route: "live",
      files: [{ name: "zdjęcie 1.png", size: 1000, type: "image/png" }],
    });
    const ticket = upload.body.files[0] as SingleTicket;
    expect(await put(ticket.put, offsetBytes(1000))).toBe(200);
    expect((await call<CompleteResponse>("complete", { token: ticket.token })).status).toBe(200);
    expect((await cloudrop.api.head(ticket.key)).data).toMatchObject({
      size: 1000,
      type: "image/png",
      disposition: "inline; filename=\"zdj_cie 1.png\"; filename*=UTF-8''zdj%C4%99cie%201.png",
      metadata: { "cloudrop-id": ticket.fileId },
    });
    const link = await cloudrop.api.getUrl(ticket.key, { expiresIn: 300, download: "photo.png" });
    const response = await fetch(link.data?.url ?? "");
    expect({
      status: response.status,
      disposition: response.headers.get("content-disposition"),
      bytes: new Uint8Array(await response.arrayBuffer()),
    }).toEqual({
      status: 200,
      disposition: "attachment; filename=\"photo.png\"; filename*=UTF-8''photo.png",
      bytes: offsetBytes(1000),
    });
  });

  it("resumes a multipart upload: complete names the missing part, sign renews it, complete succeeds", async () => {
    const size = 8 * MiB + 5;
    const bytes = offsetBytes(size);
    const upload = await call<UploadResponse>("upload", {
      route: "live",
      files: [{ name: "video.bin", size, type: "application/octet-stream" }],
    });
    const ticket = upload.body.files[0] as MultipartTicket;
    const [, second] = ticket.parts as [SignedPart, SignedPart];
    openUploads.push({ key: ticket.key, uploadId: tokenPayload(ticket.token).uploadId as string });
    expect(await put(second, bytes.slice(8 * MiB))).toBe(200);
    const incomplete = await call<CompleteResponse>("complete", { token: ticket.token });
    expect({ status: incomplete.status, parts: incomplete.body.error?.parts }).toEqual({ status: 409, parts: [1] });
    const renewed = await call<SignResponse>("sign", { token: ticket.token, parts: [1] });
    const [first] = (renewed.body as { parts: SignedPart[] }).parts;
    expect(await put(first as SignedPart, bytes.slice(0, 8 * MiB))).toBe(200);
    const done = await call<CompleteResponse>("complete", { token: ticket.token });
    expect(done.status).toBe(200);
    expect((await cloudrop.api.head(ticket.key)).data?.size).toBe(size);
    // A retried completion after R2 forgot the upload succeeds by checking the stored object (spec §5.4).
    expect((await call<CompleteResponse>("complete", { token: ticket.token })).status).toBe(200);
  });

  it("aborts a multipart upload on R2", async () => {
    const upload = await call<UploadResponse>("upload", {
      route: "live",
      files: [{ name: "big.bin", size: 9 * MiB, type: "application/octet-stream" }],
    });
    const ticket = upload.body.files[0] as MultipartTicket;
    const uploadId = tokenPayload(ticket.token).uploadId as string;
    openUploads.push({ key: ticket.key, uploadId });
    expect((await call("abort", { token: ticket.token })).status).toBe(200);
    expect((await storage?.listParts(ticket.key, uploadId))?.error?.code).toBe("NO_SUCH_UPLOAD");
  });

  it("erases a folder with deletePrefix and proves it empty", async () => {
    const folder = `${prefix}erase/`;
    await Promise.all(["a.txt", "b.txt"].map((name) => cloudrop.api.put(`${folder}${name}`, "x")));
    expect((await cloudrop.api.deletePrefix(folder)).data?.deleted).toBe(2);
    expect((await storage?.list(folder))?.data?.items).toEqual([]);
  });

  it("reports bucket settings an object token may not read as unknown", async () => {
    // Measured for CORS in the spike (Q8: GetBucketCors answers 403 to an Object Read & Write token).
    const report = await cloudrop.api.checkBucket({ origins: ["http://localhost:5173"] });
    expect([report.data?.cors.status, report.data?.lifecycle.status]).toEqual(["unknown", "unknown"]);
  });
});
```

Bugs these tests catch, beyond the obvious ones:

- Signed headers the real R2 stores differently from the fake (type, disposition, `cloudrop-id`).
- `response-content-disposition` not applied by R2 (measured: it is).
- An idempotent retry of `/complete` that works on the fake but not against R2's `NoSuchUpload`.
- `checkBucket` reporting "problem" for settings an object token may not read.

1. Replace:

```md
- `packages/cloudrop` has zero runtime dependencies and uses only web-platform APIs in `src/`. The compiler enforces
  it: `tsconfig.json` has `types: []` and excludes tests; `tsconfig.test.json` adds Node types for tests.
- Tests live next to the source (`foo.ts` beside `foo.test.ts`); there is no `test/` folder.
- The lane is chosen by filename: `*.test.ts` is the unit lane (`bun run test`); `*.integration.test.ts` runs only
  through its lane script and fails, never skips, when its service or credentials are missing.
- Public calls return `{ data, error }`. Error messages say what happened and what to do next; they never contain
  credentials, presigned URLs or raw storage responses.
- Never print, log or commit credentials, and never log a presigned URL.
- Live R2 tests write only under `live-test/cloudrop-<random>/` and delete what they create.
```

   with:

```md
- `packages/cloudrop` has zero runtime dependencies and uses only web-platform APIs in `src/`. The compiler enforces
  it: `tsconfig.json` has `types: []` and excludes tests; `tsconfig.test.json` adds Node types for tests.
  `src/node.ts` describes Node's request and response by hand for the same reason; `src/node.test.ts` proves real Node
  objects fit them.
- Tests live next to the source (`foo.ts` beside `foo.test.ts`); there is no `test/` folder. Server tests run the real

- The lane is chosen by filename: `*.test.ts` is the unit lane (`bun run test`); `*.integration.test.ts` runs only
  through its lane script and fails, never skips, when its service or credentials are missing.
- Public calls return `{ data, error }`. Error messages say what happened and what to do next; they never contain
  credentials, presigned URLs or raw storage responses. The handler answers every request, never throws, and sends a
  client only the error's code, message, `retryable` and part numbers; everything else goes to the logger.
- The server trusts only the upload token after `/upload`: never a key, size, type or upload ID from a request body.
- Never print, log or commit credentials, and never log a presigned URL.
- Live R2 tests write only under `live-test/cloudrop-<random>/` and delete what they create.
```

````md
- Tested against real R2 and several S3-compatible servers.

## Server (in development)

```ts
// lib/cloudrop.ts
import { createCloudrop, r2, route, unauthorized } from "cloudrop";

export const cloudrop = createCloudrop({
  storage: r2({ bucket: "media" }), // CLOUDROP_R2_ACCOUNT_ID, _ACCESS_KEY_ID, _SECRET_ACCESS_KEY from the environment
  // secret: CLOUDROP_SECRET from the environment (openssl rand -base64 32)
  routes: {
    avatar: route({
      accept: "image/*",
      maxSize: "5MB",
      authorize: async ({ request }) => {
        const user = await getUser(request);
        return user ? { userId: user.id } : unauthorized();
      },
      prefix: ({ userId }) => `users/${userId}/avatars`,
    }),
  },
});

// app/api/cloudrop/[...all]/route.ts
import { toNextHandler } from "cloudrop/next";
export const { GET, POST, OPTIONS } = toNextHandler(cloudrop);
```

`cloudrop.handler(request)` serves `/api/cloudrop/{upload,sign,complete,abort}` in any framework that speaks
`Request` and `Response`; `cloudrop/node` bridges Node's `http`. `cloudrop.api` reads, writes, copies and deletes
objects on the same storage, and `cloudrop.api.checkBucket()` reports CORS and lifecycle problems.

Background research: [`docs/research`](docs/research).
````

6. Replace:

```md
| Method | Behaviour |
|---|---|
| `getUrl(key, { expiresIn, download })` | Public domain: plain URL. Private: presigned GET whose expiry is rounded to a time window, so every viewer in that window gets an identical, cacheable URL. `download` sets the filename. |
| `head(key)` | `{ size, type, etag, uploadedAt, metadata }`; `null` only on 404. Network errors are errors, never "not found". |
| `put(key, body, { type })` | Small server-side upload (Blob, ArrayBuffer, string) up to the single-PUT limit. |
| `delete(key)`, `deleteMany(keys)` | Any number of keys, one DeleteObject request per key, 16 at a time, with per-key results `{ deleted, failed[] }`. |
| `copy(from, to)` | Server-side; `UploadPartCopy` above 5 GiB. |
| `deletePrefix(prefix)` | Deletes page by page (one request per key, through `deleteMany`), re-lists to prove nothing remains, returns the proof. For erasure requests. |
| `checkBucket()` | Report on CORS (`ETag` exposed), the abort-incomplete lifecycle rule and public-domain conflicts. |

## 10. Lessons carried over from earlier implementations
```

   with:

```md
| Method | Behaviour |
|---|---|
| `getUrl(key, { expiresIn, download })` | Public domain and no options: plain URL. Otherwise a presigned GET signed at the start of an `expiresIn` window (default 3600 s) and valid for two windows, so every viewer in that window gets an identical, cacheable URL that stays valid at least `expiresIn` seconds. `download` sets the filename through a signed `response-content-disposition`, also on a public bucket. |
| `head(key)` | `{ size, type, etag, uploadedAt, metadata }`; `null` only on 404. Network errors are errors, never "not found". |
| `put(key, body, { type })` | Small server-side upload (Blob, ArrayBuffer, string) up to the single-PUT limit. |
| `delete(key)`, `deleteMany(keys)` | Any number of keys, one DeleteObject request per key, 16 at a time, with per-key results `{ deleted, failed[] }`. |
| `copy(from, to)` | Server-side; `UploadPartCopy` above 5 GiB. |
| `deletePrefix(prefix)` | A folder prefix ending in `/`, so `users/4/` never matches `users/42/`. Deletes page by page (one request per key, through `deleteMany`), re-lists to prove nothing remains, returns `{ deleted, verifiedAt }`. Fails, never loops, when deleted keys reappear or writers keep adding keys (10,000 pages). For erasure requests. |
| `checkBucket({ origins })` | Report on CORS for the origins (default `trustedOrigins`: `PUT` and the signed headers allowed, `ETag` exposed), the abort-incomplete lifecycle rule and public-domain conflicts. Each check is `ok`, `problem` or `unknown` (credentials that may not read the setting). |
```

### Placeholder scan

Searched the plan for "TBD", "TODO", "add validation", "similar to Task", "write tests for", "handle errors" and
"implement later": none. Every code step holds the complete file or exact find-and-replace edits whose `old` text was
checked to occur once. The only values an implementer fills in are re-measurements (Tasks 3, 8, 14), each with the
2026-11-15 measurement already written in.

### Type and name consistency

The code in this plan was implemented and run before the plan was written. Every task's files were then replayed in
order on a fresh copy of `main` at `b0f3e9a`. After each task, `biome check`, both typechecks, the unit lane and the
build passed, and the final tree matched the implementation byte for byte. The names used across tasks therefore
resolve: `ServerConfig`, `RouteConfig`, `EndpointInput`, `UploadTokenPayload`, `MultipartToken`, `TokenKeys`,
`PluginFile`, `AbortContext`, `Logger`, `FileTicket`, `SignedRequest`, `SignedPart`, `UploadedFile`, `BucketReport`
and `FileApi`. The live R2, RustFS and Wrangler lanes were run against that final tree on 2026-11-15.

### Every test can go red

Each test step names the bugs its tests catch. These mutations were applied one at a time to the finished code, and
each was caught by the named file:

| Mutation | Caught by |
|---|---|
| `exp` check `>=` → `>` | token › "reports a token as expired from its exp second on" |
| signature comparison always equal | token › rotated-out and unknown-secret rows |
| body cap `>` → `>=` | handler › "reads a body of exactly 65,536 bytes" |
| trusted-origin check removed | handler › "refuses another site…", "refuses the app over http…" |
| JSON content-type check removed | handler › text/plain and form rows |
| `isUnauthorized` check removed | upload › "refuses with authorize's message and starts nothing" |
| `maxSize` off by one | upload › "accepts a file of exactly maxSize and refuses one byte more" |
| NFC removed | upload › "normalizes the key the prefix built to NFC" |
| `image/*` prefix without the slash | accept › "does not let image/* match a type that only starts with the same letters" |
| SVG served inline | key › "serves image/svg+xml as attachment" |
| URL lifetime not clamped | sign › "never signs a URL that outlives its token" |
| size check dropped at completion | complete › "discards an object with a different size…" |
| `cloudrop-id` check dropped at completion | complete › "never deletes an object another writer stored…" |
| `cloudrop-id` check dropped at abort | abort › "keeps an object another writer stored…" |
| `deletePrefix` trailing-slash rule dropped | api › empty-prefix and no-slash rows |
| survivor check dropped | api › "fails instead of looping…" |
| earlier plugin not released | upload › "lets a plugin refuse the request and releases…" |
| duplicate part numbers allowed | sign › "refuses a repeated part" |
| Node adapter reads the whole body | node › "reads at most one chunk past 64 KiB…" (this mutant survived the first draft's tests; that case was added for it) |
| ETag check dropped from HEAD | s3-driver › "reports a HEAD answer without an ETag…" |
| development check not run once | api › "checks the bucket once, on the first request in development…" |

The token's "a second spelling of the same signature" row was red against the first implementation, which compared
decoded bytes; the implementation was changed to compare base64url text.

### Not settled here (for the owner)

- CI's `setup-node` installs Node 22 while `engines` says `>=24` (commit `dcae90e`), and spec §3 still says "Node
  22+". M2 changes neither.
- `biome.json` declares schema 2.6.7 while Biome 2.6.8 runs (an info message on every check). M2 does not change it.
- D4 (no second `authorize` at `/sign` and `/complete`) and D20 (an owner may delete their own completed single
  upload through `/abort` within 24 hours) are trade-offs the spec leaves open; both are documented in code and here.
